And the inheritance that was once the Lord's, the people of the Jews, became like a lion in the forest against him, when in the passion it cried out against him with one voice. And because it gave forth its voice against him, therefore he held it in hatred and cast it off, and she who was once beloved and most dear is now called hateful.

VIII. Is my inheritance to me a bird of many colors? Is it a bird dyed all over? Come, gather together, all you beasts of the earth, hasten to devour! LXX: Is my inheritance to me a hyena's cave? Or a cave round about it, upon it? Go, gather all the beasts of the field, and let them come to eat it! 2. By the bird of many colors and dyed all over he means, according to the letter, the peacock. 'Such beauty', he says, 'did Israel have, and with such virtues was Jerusalem adorned, that there was nothing good that was not seen in her. Because, therefore, what was once my inheritance, that is, the people of Israel, has become to me like a lion in the forest and has given forth its voice against me, and I have detested it with all hatred, therefore come and gather together against it, all you beasts of the earth, the multitude of diverse nations, and devour her who has not known her Lord!' 3. But if, as the Seventy and the other interpreters translated, it is read: Is my inheritance to me a hyena's cave?, let us refer it to the uncleanness of that nocturnal beast, which lives on the corpses of the dead and is wont to dig bodies out of tombs, and there is no uncleanness on which it does not feed. Such is Israel, offending its Lord and handed over to the bites of all the beasts.

IX. Many shepherds have demolished — or have corrupted — my vineyard, they have trampled — or have polluted — my part, they have made my desirable portion a desert of solitude — or pathless —. They have made it — or it has been made — a ruin, and it has mourned over me or has perished. 2. Let those hear these things who desire to be princes of the peoples, and who are to render account in the day of judgment not only for themselves but also for the flocks entrusted to them; for because of them the part of the Lord is trampled and polluted, so that where once was the lodging of God, there is the dwelling-place of beasts. Others, however, understand not the leaders of the people and the priests, but the princes of the enemies, who laid Jerusalem, that is, the vineyard of the Lord, in ruins.

X. And it has mourned over me; with desolation the whole land has been made desolate, because there is no one who considers in his heart. Upon all the ways of the desert the destroyers have come, because the sword of the Lord has devoured from the end of the earth to its end; there is no peace for all flesh. LXX: because of me with ruin the land has been ruined, and the rest. 2. This, which we have set down: and it has mourned over me, is joined according to the Hebrew to the preceding verse, so that it reads: they have made it a ruin, that is, my inheritance, and it has mourned over me, bereft of my help. According to the Seventy, however, God says that because of him the land has been ruined and reduced to a solitude, because there is no one who considers in his heart, nor is there any peace for all flesh. For flesh cannot receive the peace of God; for the wisdom of the flesh is hostile to God, and those who are in the flesh cannot please God. 3. Further, according to the Hebrew, all Judea has been made desolate for this reason, that there is no one who considers God in his heart, nor anyone left who can escape. For through all the ways of the wilderness the destroyers have come, that is, the hostile army, and the sword of the Lord has devoured from border to border, and there was no rest for those fleeing from the city. Hence it is said: there is no peace for all flesh.

XI. They have sown wheat and reaped thorns; they have received an inheritance, and it will not profit them. LXX: You have sown wheat and reaped thorns; their clergy will not profit them. 'They expected better things', he says, 'and the worst came; they hoped for prosperity and suffered adversity; they received from the Lord an abundance of all things, which will not profit them'. 2. According to the Seventy, however, all the heretics sow as it were wheat and reap thorns, while the Lord waited for them to bear fruit; and they did not do judgment, but a cry. This is said also to churchmen, who ruin the words of the Lord and his teaching by their evil manner of life, concerning whom it is added: their clergy will not profit them; for what can the name of bishop and of presbyter, or the rest of the ecclesiastical order, help them, when they are rather weighed down by their dignities, and the mighty suffer torments mightily, and the more has been entrusted to them, the more is required of them?

XII. Be confounded at the fruits — or at your boasting — because of the wrath of the fury of the Lord — or at the reproach in the sight of the Lord —. Thus says the Lord against all my most wicked neighbors, who touch the inheritance which I distributed to my people Israel: Behold, I will pluck them up from their land, and I will pluck up — or cast out — the house of Judah from the midst of them. And when I have plucked them up, I will turn — or return — and have mercy on them and bring them back — or make them dwell —, each man in his inheritance and each man in his land. 2. It is said to those whom their clergy and the ecclesiastical order will not profit, that they should be confounded at their boasting and at the reproach before the Lord. And as to what he adds: against all my most wicked neighbors: according to the letter, the neighbors of the holy land are the Edomites, the Philistines, Moab and Ammon; but according to tropology, they are all the heretics, who are reckoned under the name of Christ and are neighbors rather than inhabitants of the holy land, who touch the inheritance of God and lay it waste; and it is said of them that they are removed from the midst of the land and that the house of Judah is taken out of their midst. These, when they have been plucked up and freed from the jaws of the heretics, will obtain the mercy of God and will be brought back into their inheritance and into their land.

XIII. And it shall be, if, being instructed, they learn the ways of my people, so as to swear in my name: The Lord lives, as they taught my people to swear by Baal, they shall be built up in the midst of my people; but if they do not hear, I will pluck up that nation with plucking up and destruction, says the Lord. If those brought over from heresy into the Church learn the ways of the people of God and swear in the name of the Lord, and not in the name of the idols which they fashioned out of their own heart, they will be built up by the Lord and will be part of his people. 2. But if, having been brought over into the Church, they keep remnants of perverse dogmas and do not hear the words of the Lord, that nation will be plucked up from the midst of the people of God with a perpetual plucking up and destruction, so that no place of repentance whatever is left to them. These things we see daily and prove by the facts themselves: that the heretics feign the truth of the faith for this reason, that they may deceive all the simple, not that they themselves may be converted to the faith, but that they may draw the faithful into unbelief.

Jeremiah 13

XIV.

Thus says the Lord to me: Go and get yourself a loincloth — or girdle — of linen, and you shall put it upon your loins, and you shall not bring it into water — or it shall not pass through water —! And I got the loincloth according to the word of the Lord and put it about my loins. And the word of the Lord came to me a second time, saying: Take the loincloth — or girdle —, which you have got, which is about your loins, and arise, go to the Euphrates and hide it there in a cleft of the rock! 2. And I went and hid it in the Euphrates, as the Lord had commanded me. And it came to pass after very many days, the Lord said to me: Arise, go to the Euphrates and take from there the loincloth — or girdle —, which I commanded you to hide there! And I went to the Euphrates and dug and took the loincloth from the place where I had hidden it; and behold, the loincloth — or girdle — had rotted, so that it was fit for no use. 3. And the word of the Lord came to me, saying: Thus says the Lord: So will I make the pride — or injury — of Judah rot, and the great pride of Jerusalem, and this most wicked people, who refuse to hear my words and walk in the perversity — or in the direction — of their most wicked heart, and have gone after strange gods, to serve them and to worship them; and they shall be like this loincloth, which is fit for no use. 4. For as the loincloth clings to the loins of a man, so I glued to myself the whole house of Israel and the whole house of Judah, says the Lord, that it might be to me for a people and for a name and for praise and for glory; and they did not hear. 5. The girdle or loincloth, which is joined to the loins of God, is the people of Israel, which, taken up from the earth in the likeness of flax and unwashed, had neither softness nor whiteness, and yet through his mercy clung to God. And when it had sinned — for linen and a loincloth of this kind is rational —, it was led across the Euphrates, that is, to the Assyrians, and there it is hidden, that is, in a manner swallowed up by the multitude of great and innumerable nations and reckoned as nothing. 6. But after a long time the prophet himself, as a type of God, frees the people from captivity. And this people nevertheless, even after the return, did not keep the precepts of God, but, following strange gods, at the last even laid hands on the Son of God and wasted away in eternal perdition. Every holy man too is a loincloth of God, who, taken up from the earth and from the clay of the earth, is joined in fellowship with God and, in a manner, covers and girds with greater diligence the things in his Church that seem shameful, lest they lie open to the bites of the Gentiles and the heretics. 7. This loincloth, if it touches water and crosses the streams of the Euphrates, so as to be soaked with the moisture of the Assyrian region, loses its former strength and rots and falls apart; and although it returns to the use of God, yet it cannot have its former beauty, not through the harshness of God but through its own fault, because they refuse to hear his words and walk in the perversity of their heart, or what seems right to them, this they do. 8. But the divine word itself also explains why it set forth this likeness, saying: 'For as the loincloth clings to the loins of a human being, so I glued and made to cling to me the whole house of Israel and the whole house of Judah' — namely the ten and the two tribes —, 'that it might be to me for a people named and for praise and for glory; and for all these things they did not hear me, but followed their own vices'. 9. Let him therefore beware who can say: But for me it is good to cling to God, lest perhaps through negligence he be separated from his loins and cross the Euphrates and be given into the power of the king of Assyria, and be lodged by no means in the most solid rock but in a cleft of a rock corrupted and vitiated, that is, in the filth and vices of the heretics, and come into such rottenness that he can no longer return to the use of the Lord and to being his girdle.

XV. You shall say therefore to them — or to the people — this word: Thus says the Lord God of Israel: Every flask — or wineskin — shall be filled with wine. And they will say to you: Do we not know that every flask — or wineskin — is filled with wine? And you shall say to them: Thus says the Lord: Behold, I will fill all the inhabitants of this land, and the kings who sit of the stock — or the sons — of David upon his throne, and the priests and the prophets and all the inhabitants of Jerusalem with drunkenness, and I will scatter them, a man from his brother, and fathers and sons together, says the Lord; I will not spare — or I will not long for — and I will not yield nor have mercy, so as not to destroy them. The Hebrew word 'nebel' Aquila's first edition translated 'flask', the second 'nebel' itself, Symmachus 'mixing-bowl', the Seventy 'wineskin', Theodotion 'vessel'; and all this is filled not with oil, not with water, not with honey, not with milk, nor with any other matter of a liquid element, but with wine and drunkenness, showing that we are a fragile vessel, according to the Apostle, who says: But we have this treasure in earthen vessels, and that it cannot be that there should not be fulfilled in us what is written: For good does not dwell in my flesh, and again: For the good that I will, this I do not do, but the evil that I do not will, this I work, and then: Wretched man that I am, who will deliver me from the body of this death? 3. And with this drunkenness, by which we forget the precepts of God, and with vices and sins, the whole human condition is filled, as the prophet says: No one living shall be justified in your sight — not by comparison with God, as the old and new heretics and the patrons of the heretics would have it, but with respect to his knowledge; for man sees in the face, God in the heart — and what sometimes seems clean to us is found filthy in his eyes: not only the ignoble crowd and the worthless rabble, but the kings of the churches of the stock or sons of David, who sit leaning back, with neck erect and paunch thrust out, upon his throne. 4. The priests too themselves, the second rank in ecclesiastical honor, and the prophets, who seem to have knowledge of the Scriptures, and all the inhabitants of Jerusalem are filled according to the variety of their sins, or Judah, as the Seventy added. And when they are drunk, they are scattered from fellowship with one another, and fathers are separated from sons and sons from fathers, so that they are defiled by diverse heresies and under the name of Christ fight one another with the sword and do battle against their mother who bore them, the Church. 5. Hence he says: 'I will not long for them, but I will hold them in everlasting hatred; I will not spare and I will not yield nor have mercy, not out of cruelty of sentence but out of the truth of judgment. For those who slaughtered my peoples will themselves perish forever'. This can also be taken simply according to the history: that the kings and the priests and the prophets and all the people of Jerusalem were to be made drunk with the Babylonian cup and overwhelmed by the evils of captivity.

XVI. Hear and perceive with your ears, do not be lifted up, for the Lord has spoken! Because he had said above: Every wineskin shall be filled with wine, so that the kings too and the priests and the prophets and every inhabitant of Jerusalem should be filled with drunkenness, therefore he joins on and says: Hear and perceive with your ears, as much outwardly as inwardly, as much with the mind as with the body, and do not be lifted up through pride, considering your frailty and that there is no one who, according to the quality of sin, is free of this drunkenness. Hence he who lifts himself up against him through pride is destroyed and corrupted and is unworthy of the mercy of God.

XVII. Give glory to the Lord our God, before it grows dark and before your feet stumble upon the misty — or dark — mountains! You will wait for light, and there will be the shadow of death, and they will be placed in darkness — or, according to the Hebrew: and he will set it in the shadow of death and in gloom —. But if you do not hear, in secret the soul will weep, mine — or yours — before the face of pride or injury. Those to whom the divine word had said: 2. Hear and perceive with your ears and do not be lifted up, he now calls to repentance, that, before they are led to Babylon and their feet stumble upon the dark or misty mountains, they should give glory to God. Hence sinners too are often told: Give glory to God! 3. And that Babylon and the whole region of the Chaldeans are called misty or dark mountains we read at the beginning of Isaiah, against Babylon, where it is written: Upon the misty mountain lift up a sign, which in Hebrew is called 'neseptha'. This therefore he commands: that, before they are led into captivity and feel the evils of servitude, they should do repentance, since, when they wait for light, they are to sit in darkness. 4. 'But if', he says, 'you refuse to hear me, secretly' — according to Aquila 'in darkness' — 'the soul will weep, mine' — or, according to the Seventy, 'yours' — 'before the face of pride, so that not even groaning and weeping may be free, lest the eyes of the victors be offended'. But we can also interpret this passage thus: the Savior speaks: 'Work while it is day; the night will come, when no one can work any longer'. 5. Of this time there is also the prophecy of Isaiah: For the stars of heaven and Orion and all the adornment of heaven will not give light, and they will be darkened at the rising of the sun, and the moon will not give light. Zephaniah too agrees in the same words, saying: A day of tribulation and distress, a day of misery and perdition, a day of darkness and whirlwind, a day of cloud and gloom. 6. Before, therefore, the time of judgment arrives and our feet stumble upon the dark mountains, namely the adverse powers that are set over torments and tortures, let us do repentance, lest, waiting for light, we be wrapped in the darkness of night; and let us know that, unless we do this, the soul either of God or of the prophet will weep before the face of our pride, who refuse to hear the words of God. Hence the prophet himself also says:

XVIII. Weeping, Jerusalem shall weep — or weeping, it shall weep — and my eye shall shed a tear, and I cannot conceal my grief in silent groans; but the whole cause of the torments is that the flock of the Lord has been taken captive. 2. Let us say to the Jews and to our own Judaizers, who follow only the simple and killing history: Unless you hear secretly, that is, in mystery or in darkness, which God has made his hiding place, and, according to Solomon, so as to understand a parable and a dark saying, the soul will weep, the prophet's or their own, before the face of pride, while through contumacy they resist God. Hence there will also be continual weeping and perpetual tears, because the flock of the Lord has been taken captive and corrupted by the true Nebuchadnezzar.

XIX. Say to the king and to the mistress — or say to the king and to the mighty —: Humble yourselves, sit down, for there has come down — or there has been taken away — from your head the crown of your glory! The cities of the south are shut up, and there is none who opens; all Judea has been carried away — or all Judah has been carried away — with exile complete — or captivity —. 2. The prophet is commanded to speak to King Jeconiah and to his mother, whom he calls lady and mistress or queen, that they should humble themselves and sit in the dust; for they have lost the royal dignity and are to be handed over to the Babylonian king. The cities of the south are shut up, that is, the tribe of Judah and Jerusalem, which, lying next to the desert, faces toward the south, and there is none who opens them, surrounded as they are by siege. 3. All Judea has been carried away — or all Judah — with exile complete, or 'it has received what it deserved, and it has been fulfilled in it'; for so the Seventy translated. He raves in this place who understands the king as Christ and the mighty as angels or apostles, as if they were to take on the body of humility and sit in the dust and lose — either the king or the mighty — from their head the crown and the glory of strength. 4. He also interprets the shut cities of the south as hell, as cities opened to no one, and says that all the glory of Judah was carried away when there was fulfilled in the passion: All have turned aside, together they have become useless; there is none who does good, there is not even one. The Hebrew word 'gebira' Aquila and Symmachus translated 'mistress' and 'lady'; the Seventy thought it was 'geburoth' and said 'the mighty'.

XX. Lift up your eyes and see those who are coming — or you who are coming — from the north! Where is the flock that was given to you, your glorious herd? What will you say when he visits you? For you teach them against yourself and instruct them to your own head. 2. The inhabitants of Jerusalem are commanded to lift up their eyes and see the Chaldeans coming from the region of the north; and the city herself is questioned, and it is said to her: 'Where is the flock that was given to you, your glorious herd? Where is your people, which you had received from God? Where is that multitude, so great and glorious that you would have believed the crowd of a whole province gathered into one place? 3. What will you say when he visits you, the Lord, with his rod, and hands you over to the Babylonian enemies, whom you yourself taught against you, either to your own head or from the beginning, so that you fled to their help and followed their idols — they who, under the pretext of your friendship, learned by what road they should come to you?' Let the negligent Church hear this, that she herself teaches her adversaries how they can seize her in a spiritual captivity and tear her herd with the cruelty of beasts.

XXI. Will not pains seize you like a woman in labor? But if you say in your heart: Why have these things come upon me? Because of the multitude of your iniquity your more shameful parts have been uncovered, your soles have been polluted — or have been dishonored —. 2. 'While you do not know it, as a sudden childbirth seizes a woman, so a sudden captivity will seize you. But if you wish to complain and ask why you have been handed over to enemies, hear plainly that the multitude of your iniquity has done these things to you, so that, as of a harlot woman whose garments have been lifted, your ignominy has been uncovered and your fornications have been shown in public'. 3. By these things we learn that, so long as sins are lesser, God acts patiently and waits for our repentance; but if we choose to join offenses to offenses and to make a heap of sins, our shameful parts will be uncovered and our soles will be shown to all, either in the present age or in the future. For there is nothing hidden that will not be made manifest, when that word of Daniel will be fulfilled: These shall rise to eternal life and those to reproach and to everlasting confusion.

XXII. If the Ethiopian can change his skin or the leopard its spots, you too will be able to do good, when you have learned evil. This testimony is used against the Church by those who desire to assert diverse natures, and they say that the blackness or the variegation of sins is so great that they cannot pass into whiteness and the beauty of a single color, not attending to what follows: and you will be able to do good, when you have learned evil. 2. For whatever is learned is not of nature but of effort and of one's own will, which through excessive habit and love of sinning is in a manner turned into nature. But this, which is impossible for men, is possible for God: so that it is by no means the Ethiopian and the leopard who are seen to change their nature, but he who works in the Ethiopian and the leopard, as the Apostle says: I can do all things in him who strengthens me, Christ. 3. Hence also in another place: I labored, he says, more than all of them; yet not I, but the grace of God which is in me, and: I live, now not I, but Christ lives in me. And again we read it written: What do you have that you did not receive? But if you received it, why do you glory as if you had not received it? For these reasons let not the wise man glory in his wisdom, nor the strong man in his strength, nor the rich man in his riches, nor the chaste man in his chastity, knowing that in all these things it is the power of Christ, not of those who glory in their virtues.

XXIII. And I will scatter them like stubble that is snatched away by the wind in the desert. This is your lot and the portion of your measure from me, says the Lord or and the portion of your disobedience against me. 'Because through excessive habit of evils they could not change their nature — not by the fault of the Creator, but by the pursuit of ingrained wickedness —, therefore I will scatter them like stubble snatched away by the wind into the wilderness, according to what is written elsewhere: like the dust which the wind casts from the face of the earth'. 2. And he makes an apostrophe to Jerusalem herself: that this is her lot and this the portion which she herself has chosen, a measure heaped up and full and running over, or the portion of her disobedience, by which she would not acquiesce to the Lord; for with what measure she has measured, it will be measured back to her.

XXIV. Because you have forgotten me and trusted — or hoped — in falsehood, therefore I too have bared — or will bare and will uncover — your thighs — and hinder parts — before your face, and your ignominy will appear, your adulteries and your neighing, the wickedness — or estrangement — of your fornication. 2. The cause of the scattering of Jerusalem is that she has forgotten God and trusted or hoped in falsehoods. He who, apart from God, trusts in the things of the world forgets God. Hence his thighs or hinder parts are uncovered, so that he may see his own ignominy, and the things that ought to be behind may come to the front, and he himself may perceive what he has done, and his ignominy may appear not only to himself but to all. 3. Your adulteries, he says, and your neighing shows not lust alone but the madness of lust, after the manner of mares that are eager for coupling, as in that line of Vergil: hippomanes, as the shepherds call it by a true name, a sticky venom drips from the groin. Let us ask Jesus that neither in the present age nor in the future he uncover our thighs and hinder parts, but that he blot out our iniquities and not let all our crimes appear.

XXV. Upon the hills in the field I have seen your abominations. Woe to you, Jerusalem! You will not be cleansed — or because you have not been cleansed — after me; how long yet? 'Not only in the midst of the city of Jerusalem but on every hill and in all regions I have seen your idols'; hence it is said to her: 'Woe to you, Jerusalem, because you have not been cleansed after me, so that, though you boasted that you followed my footsteps and paraded the confession of my name, yet you have never been purified, because you have forgotten me and hoped in falsehoods'. 2. Hence he rebukes her and says: How long yet? And the sense is: 'How long shall I wait for you? How long shall I bear it? How long will you forget me to the end and despise my precepts?' He commits fornication on the hills and in the fields and is never cleansed, who, with neck erect through pride, is not humbled under the mighty hand of God but trusts in his own crimes and vices.

Jeremiah 14

XXVI.

The word of the Lord which came to Jeremiah concerning the words of the drought. All things are in accord with the wrath of God; hence the sun also sets at noon upon sinners, and the moon and the other stars do not give their light, and we must suppose that in the time of the siege there were no rains, so that the besieged endured a dearth of water. For the city uses a single spring, that of Siloam, and this not a perennial one, and to the present day a dearth of rains causes a lack not only of crops but also of water to drink.

XXVII. Judea has mourned — or Judah — and its gates have fallen — or have been made empty — and have been darkened — or have been made dark — upon the earth, and the cry of Jerusalem has gone up. Its elders sent their lesser ones — or younger ones — to the water; they came to draw — or to the wells — and did not find water; they brought back their vessels empty. They were confounded and afflicted — or they blushed — and covered their heads; because of the desolation of the land — or and the works of the land, because they have failed —, because no rain came — or there was no rain — upon the land, the farmers were confounded, they covered their heads. 2. In the time of drought, when the multitude suffers a famine of hearing and learning the word of God, Judea mourns, 'boasting' that it formerly had the worship of God and the 'confession' of the true faith; and its gates are either emptied or fall, which we ought to refer to the senses, through which instruction is conceived by the soul. Then all things are darkened and wrapped in darkness, and in Jerusalem it is by no means reason and the word of teaching that reign, but clamor and confusion. The elders also, who ought themselves to go to draw the waters, send the younger ones, in whom there are no gray hairs of wisdom, and therefore they come to the wells and do not find the waters which, as the history relates, the patriarchs found. 3. They bring back their vessels empty, the younger ones, that is, not because there were no waters, but because they could not find them. They were confounded and afflicted — or they blushed — and covered their heads — because they could not say with the Apostle: 'But we all, with unveiled face, behold the glory of the Lord' — because of the desolation of the land or and the works of the land have failed, through which we advance to the knowledge of God. 4. And the cause is plain: because no rain came upon the land. For the clouds were commanded not to rain a shower upon it. The farmers also, one of whom says: You are God's husbandry, God's building and: We are God's fellow workers, cover their head and are confounded, understanding that without the grace and help of God they strive in vain.

XXVIII. For the hind also — or the hinds — gave birth in the field — or they gave birth — and forsook — or they forsook —, because there was no grass. And the wild asses stood on the crags, they drew in the wind like dragons; their eyes failed, because there was no grass or hay. 2. Great is the barrenness when even the hinds give birth in the field and forsake their young because there is no grass or hay, so that they, which by the scent of their nostrils draw serpents out of their holes and kill venomous creatures, do not enjoy the food of grace; and when the wild asses also, of which it is written: Who has sent out the wild ass free into the wilderness?, leaving the plains and the open country, stand on the crags and cannot run, and draw in the wind after the likeness of dragons, and their eyes fail and cannot see the clear light, since rational food has been withdrawn. 3. This drought often happens in the churches, when both stags and wild asses are found among the peoples and waste away through a dearth of teachers: there are those who could learn, and there are none who could teach.

XXIX. If our iniquities have answered — or have withstood — us, O Lord, act for your name's sake, for many are our turnings away — or our sins —. Against you we have sinned, O expectation of Israel, his Savior in the time of tribulation. If we are in doubt why the rains do not come down upon the land, why all things wither with dryness, let us hear: our iniquities have withstood us; 'therefore, O Lord, not according to our works but according to your holy name, overcome our many turnings away. 2. For against you we have sinned, whom the secrets of the heart do not deceive, and we wait for you, who are the true hope and expectation of Israel and save him in the time of tribulation, according to that which is written: To the Lord I cried when I was in tribulation, and he heard me'. Let us too say, in the time of drought and dearth of waters: 'Against you we have sinned and have done evil before you; we wait for your coming, you who save Israel not by its own merit but by your clemency.'

XXX. Why will you be like a settler in the land and like a traveler turning aside to lodge? Why will you be like a wandering man or a mighty one who cannot save? LXX: why have you become like a stranger in the land and like a native turning aside to lodge? Will you be like a man asleep and like a man who cannot save? 2. The Jews understand this passage thus: 'Why do you separate yourself from your people and, like a traveler, for the refreshment of a single hour not care what lodging you use, but, being about to pass on to other places, not save your people and forsake the temple once renowned?' Our people, however, think that it is said of the future dispensation of Christ: that he was to be a pilgrim in the land and to use the hospitality of the earth for a short time, and, like a man passing through and strong, having left Israel, to make for the multitude of the Gentiles, so as to pass from place to place, from people to people, from the temple to the Church. 3. But what is said according to the Seventy: Will you be like a man asleep and like a man who cannot save? sets down a likeness, not the truth of the matter, according to that which is written: Arise, why do you sleep, O Lord?, not that the Lord sleeps, of whom it is said: He who keeps Israel will not slumber nor sleep, but that he seems to sleep to those whom he forsakes. Finally, in what follows too, it is not written: 'a man asleep', who cannot save, but: 'like a man'; in both, anthrōpopathōs [in human terms].

XXXI. But you are in us, O Lord, and your name has been invoked upon us; do not forsake us or do not forget us! 'You who among the Jews will be like a stranger and a traveler and a wandering man, forsaking your ancient dwelling, dwell in us, and your name has been invoked upon us, so that we are called Christians; therefore do not forsake us nor forget us, to whom the mouths of all the prophets have sung of your future coming'.

XXXII. Thus says the Lord to this people: because it has loved — or they have loved — to move its feet and has not rested — or they have not spared — and it has not pleased the Lord — or and God was not well pleased in them —, now he will remember their iniquities and will visit their sins. 2. When the people says: 'Why have you become like a stranger and a traveler and a wanderer, so as to forsake your dwelling?', the Lord answers the people that was once his own: 'Do you wish to know the reason? Listen to what is said: because the people has loved to move its feet and has not withdrawn them from the fetter of sins, or has not rested and could not stand, therefore I too have forsaken it, and I have no good pleasure in it'. 3. He, then, who long delayed and through patience was unwilling to punish sinners, since they persisted in wickedness, will remember their iniquities and will visit the sins of those who are, as it were, sick and do not perceive God, so that they may cease to sin any more. But it should be noted in the holy Scriptures that it is always the feet of sinners that are moved, while to the saints it is said, with Moses: But you, stand here with me, and elsewhere it is written: Praise the Lord, you servants, who stand in the house of the Lord, in the courts of the house of our God!

XXXIII. And the Lord said to me: Do not pray for this people for good! When they fast, I will not hear their prayers, and if they offer burnt offerings and victims, I will not accept them, for by the sword and by famine and by pestilence I will consume them. It is foolish to pray for one who has sinned unto death, as John says: There is a sin unto death; I do not say that one should pray for that. All iniquity is sin, and there is a sin not unto death. 2. Fasts and prayers and victims and burnt offerings profit only when we withdraw from vices and weep for our former sins; but if, persisting in our crimes, we think that we can buy the ears of God with vows and sacrifices, we greatly err, judging God to be unjust. For he who has once been destined for the sword and famine and pestilence cannot be rescued by any prayers. Hence the prophet too is told not to ask in vain for what he cannot obtain.

XXXIV. And I said: Ah, ah, ah, Lord God — or you who are, Lord God —, the prophets say to them: You shall not see the sword, and famine shall not be among you, but he will give you true peace in this place. And the Lord said to me: The prophets prophesy falsely in my name. I did not send them, and I did not command them, nor did I speak to them; a lying vision and deceitful divination and the seduction of their own heart they prophesy to you. 2. Let the teachers hear this who promise prosperity to those who sin and persist in their vices, who say to the rich: 'You shall not see the sword of God's torments, and famine shall not be among you. For you will be filled with the words of God, and the Lord will give you most true peace in the place of the Church, or of Jerusalem'. But that, according to the Hebrew, he said three times: Ah, ah, ah, answers to what came before, where the Lord had threatened, saying: by the sword and by famine and by pestilence I will consume them. 3. Since, then, the prophets, nay rather the false prophets, promised false things, the Lord therefore spoke through Jeremiah: 'Do not listen to the words of the false prophets, who were not sent by me but came of their own will! Hence they are by no means to be called prophets but diviners, they who speak seduction to the people'. For it is much better to amend sins through fear of punishments than, through hope of prosperity, to fall under the divine sentence.

XXXV. Therefore thus says the Lord concerning the prophets who prophesy in my name, whom I did not send, saying: Sword and famine shall not be in this land: by sword and famine those prophets shall be consumed. And the peoples to whom they have prophesied shall be cast out in the streets of Jerusalem because of famine and sword, and there shall be none to bury them, they and their wives, their sons and their daughters, and I will pour out upon them their own evil or their own evils. 2. Let the false prophets beware, who by promising prosperity trip up the people of God, lest they themselves perish and the deceived people be destroyed by a like ruin, and they lie in the streets of Jerusalem and, trampling on the precepts of the Lord, perish by famine and sword, and there be none to bury them nor to cover their disgrace with the dust of repentance. 3. For the prophets themselves and the people, and their wives, and sons and daughters, and their whole generation will be on the dunghill without any undertaker. How many lie in the streets of Jerusalem! How many do we see unburied receiving their own evils, which they suffer as the Lord pours them out!

XXXVI. And you shall say this word to them: Let my eyes shed a tear — or let your eyes shed tears — through night and day, and let them not be silent — or let them not cease —, for the virgin daughter of my people has been crushed with a great crushing, with a most grievous blow, exceedingly. This passage is understood in two ways: either that God himself laments his people and his eyes do not cease to weep, or at any rate that he commands that the eyes of the peoples should flow with tears; and that what must be lamented is no light thing, since the virgin daughter of his people has been struck with the greatest crushing and an intolerable blow. Others think that these things are said in the person of the prophet.

XXXVII. If I go out into the fields, behold, those slain by the sword, and if I enter the city, behold, those wasted with famine — or the pain of famine —! The prophet also and the priest have gone off into a land which they did not know. A just cause for lamenting, that the virgin has been crushed, the daughter struck and the people destroyed. 'For if,' he says, 'I wish to go outside, I shall see the slain; if to enter the city, I shall behold those wasted and scarcely clinging to their bones through the necessity of famine'. 2. And what wonder is it to say this of the common people and the ignoble crowd, when the prophets also and the priests, who prophesied prosperity to others and who ought to have opened the commandments of the law, themselves went into a land which they did not know and endured the evil of captivity? Let our prophets and priests hear this: that because of their negligence there is no security either within or without, since they both scandalize those who are without and allow those who are within to perish by famine, and those who were the authors of sinning become companions in the torments.

XXXVIII. Have you utterly cast off Judah, or has your soul abhorred Zion? Why then have you struck us, so that there is no healing? We waited for peace, and there is no good, and for a time of healing, and behold, turmoil! The prophet marvels that the Lord has so suddenly cast off Judah and Jerusalem, that is, the kingdom of the two tribes, in which were the religion of God and the ceremonies of the temple, and has struck them with so great a blow that no medicine can be applied. 2. 'We waited', he says, 'for peace and for a time of healing, and there is no good at all, but, on the contrary, turmoil, so that where formerly there had been the worship of God and tranquility, there all things were filled with seditions and the roar of the enemy'. If ever, therefore, our Zion too and our Judah is cast off and the soul of God abhors it, let us by no means marvel, but rather let us say what follows:

XXXIX. We have acknowledged, O Lord, our impieties, the iniquity of our fathers, because we have sinned against you. 'Both we', he says, 'and our fathers, with the same madness, neglected the precepts of God, and the measure of our forefathers has been filled up in us, so that whatever had been lacking in them was completed by our heap'. Hence it is also said of Judas: Let the iniquity of his fathers return to remembrance in the sight of the Lord, and let not the sin of his mother be blotted out! Let it be over against the Lord always, and let the memory of them perish from the earth!

XL. Do not give us over to reproach for your name's sake, nor bring upon us the dishonor of the throne of your glory! Remember, do not make void your covenant with us! Let us consider the throne of God's glory to be not only the temple of Judea, which has often been destroyed, but every saint, in whom, according to that which is written: You have dashed his throne to the ground, it is dashed down and destroyed when he has offended God by a multitude of sins; but nevertheless he who has perished by his own fault is upheld by the clemency of the Lord, which is exchanged for the severity of the sentence if the Lord makes void his pact, by which he has promised that we shall be saved.

XLI. Are there among the graven images of the nations any that rain, or can the heavens give showers? Are you not the Lord God, whom we have awaited? For you have made all these things. After many and varied discourses he returns to the title of the prophecy, in which it is written: the word of the Lord which came to Jeremiah concerning the words of the drought. 2. So what he says is this: 'Since the images of demons cannot rain, nor can the heavens of themselves give their shower, therefore you, O Lord our God, whom we have always awaited, to whom we have turned our hopes and vows, you give your rain! For all things are yours, and whatever is good cannot be given without you, whose it is'. 3. Let us say this also against the heretics, who cannot bestow the rain of doctrines, and who, though they promise that they are heavens and boast that it is written of them: The heavens declare the glory of God, nevertheless cannot give the showers of doctrine. For it is God alone who instructs his people and bestows the diversities of grace on those who await him.

Jeremiah 15

XLII.

And the Lord said to me: if Moses and Samuel stand before me — or against me —, my soul is not toward this people. For we read that these men withstood the wrath of the Lord on behalf of the people and turned aside a sentence that was already impending. 'Even if', he says, 'they stand either in my sight or against me' — to one of whom God said: Let me alone, and I will strike this people —, 'yet I will not hear, because the crimes of the offending people are complete'.

XLIII. Cast — or send — them out from my face, and let them go forth! Sinners withdraw from God not in place but in will, although we read that both Adam and Cain were cast out from the face of God.

XLIV. And if they say to you: where shall we go forth?, you will say to them: thus says the Lord: those for death, to death, and those for the sword, to the sword, and those for famine, to famine, and those for captivity, to captivity! And I will visit upon them four kinds, says the Lord: the sword for slaying, and dogs for tearing, and the birds of the sky and the beasts of the earth for devouring and scattering. 2. The prophecy of Ezekiel also shows four plagues to which the people of the Jews was handed over: the sword, pestilence from famine, beasts, captivity. Among the beasts, moreover, understand the dogs and the birds as well, to which they were handed over for their bodies to be torn and devoured and scattered. For it could not happen that, once the Creator was neglected, the whole creation should not rise up against sinners.

XLV. And I will give them over to ferment — or commotion and distresses — to all the kingdoms of the earth because of Manasseh, son of Hezekiah, king of Judah, for all that he did in Jerusalem. We read in the book of Chronicles that Manasseh, after his captivity and repentance, returned to Jerusalem and reigned; but just as the merits of the saints descend to their posterity, as in the case of David and the rest, so the outrages of sinners reach their posterity, if their children and grandchildren have done similar things. 2. But what he says: 'I will give them over to ferment or commotion and distresses of the whole earth', was both fulfilled in part under the Babylonians and is now being fulfilled in full, since the impious people imitated a most wicked king, one who filled Jerusalem from gate to gate with the blood of the righteous. From this we learn that peoples are very often destroyed by the crime of kings and princes and of those set over them.

XLVI. For who will have pity on you, Jerusalem? or who will grieve for you? or who will go to ask for your peace? For when God has been offended no one can intercede for the outrages of sinners, because a creature cannot be as merciful as the Maker, nor can a stranger spare outsiders as the Lord spares his own.

XLVII. You have forsaken me, says the Lord; you have gone backward. The reason is given why no one should have pity on Jerusalem, or grieve for her, or entreat for her peace: that, although according to the Apostle she ought, forgetting what lies behind, to have stretched forward to what lies before, she on the contrary turned back and longed for the meats of Egypt.

XLVIII. And I will stretch out my hand over you and kill you; I have labored, being entreated — or entreating —, for which the Seventy translated: I will by no means forgive them any longer. An outstretched hand is the sign of one who strikes; the killing of sinners signifies wrath brought to completion. But what he added: I have labored, being entreated or entreating, has a twofold sense: that God has already grown faint from often forgiving them, and that he is weary from always calling them to salvation.

XLIX. And I will scatter them with a winnowing fan in the gates of the land — or of my people —; I have killed and destroyed my people, and yet they have not turned back from their ways. 'What does it profit that I am entreated so often, when they do not turn back from their most wicked ways nor do repentance? For I scattered them as with a winnowing fan, to purge my threshing floor, and I scattered them in the gates of the land, so that in a manner they might tread the thresholds of hell, and I have killed and destroyed my people, so that, compelled by the necessity of evils, they might avoid the evils that were impending'.

L. Her widows have been multiplied to me above the sand of the sea; I have brought upon them, upon the mother of the young man, a destroyer at noon; I have sent terror suddenly upon the cities. God desires to save sinners by various remedies, so that those who had despised him when he coaxed them may fear him when he threatens. 2. The widows were multiplied above the sand of the sea when their husbands had been slain; the mothers, having lost their children, felt the destroyer not at night and through ambushes, but in broad daylight, to show the open force of a stronger adversary. 'I have sent', he says, 'upon the cities' — no doubt those of Judah and of the sinful people — 'terror suddenly, so that, the more sudden the evil was, the more difficult escape would be'.

LI. She is weakened — or has cast away or has been made empty —, who bore seven — or very many —, her soul has fainted; the sun has set for her while it was still day — or midday —; she is confounded and has blushed. And her remnant I will give to the sword in the sight of their enemies, says the Lord. We have often said that the Hebrew word 'saba' means either 'seven' or 'oath' or 'very many'. Hence the translation also differs, Aquila, the Seventy and Theodotion translating 'seven', Symmachus 'very many'. 2. She, then, who was rich in children was suddenly made childless, and perished in broad daylight, and was confounded in her own desolation. 'But the remnant of the people', he says, 'I will hand over to the sword, so that no one may escape death and the wrath of God'. Others refer it to the synagogue, which was weakened so that the multitude of the Church might grow, according to what is written: The barren has borne seven — or very many — and she who had many children has been weakened. Hence also there has set for her the sun of righteousness, in whose wings is healing, and therefore she is covered with eternal confusion, losing her people by the spiritual sword.

LII. Woe is me, my mother, why did you bear me a man of strife — or of judgment —, a man of discord — or who am to be judged — in the whole earth? This can be understood synekdochikōs [by synecdoche] of Jeremiah, since he was judged not in the whole world but in the land of Judea. But it truly befits the Lord, the Savior, who says in the Gospel: For judgment I came into this world, that those who do not see may see, and those who see may become blind, of whom it is written: Behold, this one is set for the fall and the rising of many in Israel, and for a sign that will be contradicted. 2. For which of the philosophers, which of the Gentiles, which of the heretics does not judge Christ, laying down laws for him concerning his birth and passion and resurrection and substance? Nor is it a wonder that Christ, according to the reality of the body he assumed, says: Woe is me, my mother, since in another place too what is said plainly suits his person: Woe is me, for I have become like one who gathers stubble at harvest, and like a cluster at the vintage, not having an ear of grain to eat the first-fruits! 3. And lest we think that the lowliness of these groans is referred to the Word of God, who it is that laments follows at once: Woe is me, soul, for the reverent one has perished from the earth! — not that we divide the persons, as the impious do, but that one and the same Son of God speaks now according to the flesh, now according to the Word of God.

LIII. I have not lent at interest, nor has anyone lent at interest to me; all curse me, for which LXX: I have not profited, nor has anyone profited me, Theodotion: I have not owed, nor has anyone owed me. The sense of all of these, in the person of Christ, is this: 'No one has offered himself who would worthily receive my money, nor has anyone lent at interest to me by making me his debtor in cherishing the saints and the poor. Or I have not profited, nor has anyone profited me; for no one has been willing to receive as much as I desired to bestow. 2. Nor has anyone profited me; for the salvation of the creature is the gain of the Creator. Or certainly I have not owed, nor has anyone owed me; no one has given me as much as I desired to receive, nor has he made me his debtor in anything'. And what he adds: nor has anyone owed me, has this sense: 'For how could he owe me interest, who did not deign to accept a loan?' All, he says, curse me; for which of the heretics and of those who err does not curse Christ, by believing perverse things and blaspheming things more perverse?

LIV. The Lord says: if your remnant is not for good, if I did not meet you in the time of affliction and in the time of tribulation — and of distress — against the enemy. These words can also be taken in the person of Jeremiah, who was compelled to prophesy in a most evil time, with captivity already imminent, and to suffer hard things from an unbelieving people. 2. To what he had said above: Woe is me, mother, why did you bear me a man who am to be judged and contended with by all the earth? and the rest, the Lord answers: 'Do not consider the present, but the future; for your remnant and your last things will be for good. Indeed even at present, when the enemies desired to crush you, I was at your side, and you were protected by my help'. This, moreover, can be referred as well to Jeremiah as, according to the dispensation of the flesh he assumed, to the Savior. 3. In place of what we translated according to the Hebrew: all curse me, up to the place where it is written: in the time of tribulation against the enemy, I have found it written thus in the common edition: My strength has failed in those who curse me. So be it, Lord, with them being directed, if I did not stand by you in the time of their affliction and in the time of their tribulation for good against the enemy! And the sense is: 'My strength has failed in those who curse me; for they do not understand my power, which is made perfect in weakness, and the more they curse me, the more my strength fails in them'. 4. And either the prophet or the Lord joins to this and says: So be it, Lord, with them being directed! That is: 'Let the curses which the enemies speak come upon me, and let them be directed into good, if in the time of their tribulation and distress, when the enemy was laying them waste and hastening to capture them, I did not stand in your sight and entreat you for them and say: Father, forgive them; for what they do, they do not know'. But we also often find Jeremiah in this book entreating for the people.

LV. Shall iron be leagued with iron from the north, and bronze? Symmachus: Shall iron harm iron from the north, and bronze? LXX and Theodotion: If iron will know, and a covering of bronze? The cause of the variety is clear; for the word 'iare', which is written in the present place, owing to the ambiguity of its pronunciation means both 'friendship' and 'malice'; and if, in place of the letter 'res', which is similar to the letter 'daleth', 'daleth' is read, it signifies 'knowledge and understanding'. 2. What is said is to be understood thus: 'Do not grieve that the people is your enemy; for when you announce hard things, one who is hard cannot love you': either the Babylonians, who come from the north and are the hardest iron, will not be able to be joined in friendship with this people, which is harder and untamed after the likeness of bronze; or the hardest iron, that is, the people of Israel, is unworthy of the knowledge of God, since it has come to such malice that it is encased in a harder metal, bronze.

LVI. Your riches and your treasures I will give up for plunder for nothing — or without price — for all your sins and in all your borders. And I will bring your enemies — or I will make you serve your enemies — from a land which you do not know, because a fire has been kindled in my fury; it will burn upon you. 'All your substance', he says, 'I will hand over to your enemies without any price, on account of the sins which you have committed in all your borders. 2. Therefore I will bring your enemies or I will make you serve in the land of Chaldea, because my fire, once kindled in my fury, will burn in you and cannot be extinguished; for you have furnished the material for your own burning, so that my fire may consume your wood, which is in you, and hay and stubble'. And so the cause of the burning is not in the Lord, but in those who supplied fuel to the fire.

LVII. You know, Lord: remember me and visit me, and deliver me from those who persecute me! Do not take me up in your patience; know that I have borne reproach for you! Your words were found, and I ate them — or by those who reject your words; consume them — and your word became to me — or will be to me — a joy and the gladness of my heart, because your name has been called upon me, O Lord God of hosts! 2. What we said: 'You know', is not found in the Seventy. Happy, moreover, is that conscience which bears reproach for God. Hence he says: 'Your words were found, which you were speaking by my mouth, and I ate them, that is: they were turned into food for me'; or, according to Symmachus: 'I received them, so that they were turned into gladness for me, though formerly they had been a reproach'. 3. Hence the Babylonians too confess that what Jeremiah had foretold as future has been fulfilled. Or the sense is this: 'I felt distresses, I endured the miseries inflicted by a persecuting people, but nevertheless I rejoiced that I had obeyed your precepts, and for your name's sake I suffered hard things'.

LVIII. I did not sit in the council of those who make merry, and I gloried; — or I was afraid — before the face of your hand I sat alone, because you filled me with bitterness. Why has my pain become perpetual — or why are those who sadden me strengthened — and my desperate wound refused to be healed — or my wound is strong; whence shall I be healed —? It has become to me as a lie of faithless waters or as lying water that has no faithfulness. 2. The Hebrews think that these words are spoken in the person of Jerusalem: that she sat alone and was filled with bitterness, and her pain became perpetual, and that, as waters pass away, so the words of the prophets, by which they promised themselves prosperity, passed away falsely. It is better, however, that we take these words to be spoken in the person of the prophet, in the words of a holy man, who did not sit in the council or the secret gathering of those who make merry, because he feared the hand of God impending over him, or rather gloried that he had no fellowship with the wicked. 3. Alone, he says, I sat, according to what is written: I have not sat with the council of vanity, and with those who do iniquitous things I will not go in. I have hated the church of the malignant, and with the impious I will not sit; and in another place: I am solitary, until I pass over. 'Before the face', he says, 'of your hand I sat alone, while I fear you, while I always await your hand impending over me. 4. In the council of those who make merry I refused to sit, but I swallowed down my bitterness, so that I might prepare joy for myself in the future. Nor did I have intervals in my pain, but I was weighed down by continual misery, so that I expected no remedies. For those who saddened me prevailed, and my wound became strong. But in this I had consolation, that it was like lying and passing water. For as passing waters are seen while they flow, and glide away, so also every assault of the enemies has passed away, with you helping me'. 5. Would that the Lord might grant to us too not to sit in the council of those who make merry and of those who do not think of the things to come, nor to yield to adversities, but always to dread the sentence of God and to say with the prophet: I sat alone, because I am filled with bitterness! 'Let it rejoice, then, in the present time — by no means the council of the wise, but the hidden and secret company of those who make merry; for me let it be good to cleave to God, to put my hope in the Lord, to be sated with reproaches and to await the sentence of my judge, which, when the end has come, will show in deed that all sorrow and bitterness have receded like flowing waters.'

LIX. Therefore thus says the Lord: if you turn, I will turn you, and you will stand before my face; and if you separate the precious from the vile, you will be as my mouth; they will turn to you, and you will not turn to them. And I will give you to this people for a wall of bronze, strong, and they will fight against you and will not prevail, because I am with you to save you and deliver you, says the Lord. And I will free you from the hand of the most wicked and redeem you from the hand of the strong or of the pestilent. 2. It is clear that it was by no means Jerusalem but the prophet who said the preceding words. To him the Lord answers: 'If you turn the people from their sins, I too will turn you from tribulation into gladness, and you will stand before my face, as the angels stand in the sight of God, daily seeing his face, and if you separate the precious from the vile, you will be as my mouth. And do not think', he says, 'that there is no reward for a good work; if by your words you set apart any of my saints from the number of sinners, you will be as my mouth and will be joined to my precepts. For they ought to be your imitators, and not you theirs. 3. And do not shrink back and say: Why has my pain become perpetual, and my wound strong or desperate, so that I despair of being able to be healed? For I will give you as a wall of bronze, and most strong, so that you may resist the adversary with all your strength and have me as your helper, and I will free you from the hand of the most wicked or the pestilent, and redeem you either by my blood or at present by my help'. Let us consider how great a reward the word of a teacher has, if it has been able to free anyone from error and lead him out of the number of sinners!

Jeremiah 16

LX.

And the word of the Lord came to me, saying: Do not take a wife, and let there be no sons and daughters for you in this place! For thus says the Lord concerning the sons and daughters who are begotten in this place, and concerning their mothers who bore them, and concerning their fathers, of whose stock they were born in this land: They shall die by the deaths of sicknesses; they shall not be lamented and they shall not be buried; they shall be as dung upon the face of the earth, and they shall be consumed by the sword and by famine, and their corpses shall be food for the birds of heaven and for the beasts of the earth. 2. If, at the time of the impending captivity, the prophet is forbidden to marry a wife, so that he may not have tribulation of the flesh and, on top of his own grief, be tormented also by the miseries of his wife and children, how much more does the Apostle command, because the time has been shortened and the consummation is at hand, that even those who have wives should be as though they had none! 3. Hence superfluous is the reproach of the new heretic against our teaching that second and third marriages do not derive from law but from indulgence. For it is one thing to do what is good in itself, another to be granted something lest you do worse. For he himself also states the reasons why he wishes young widows to marry, adding: for some of them have already turned back after Satan. And at the same time this teacher of continence and perpetual chastity, who blasphemously makes himself equal to God, praises third and fourth marriages, which I would call not so much marriages as consolations of miseries and the last plank of the shipwrecked; unless perhaps he grants indulgence to his Amazons, so that they may wage the wars of lust right up to decrepit old age. 4. But why the prophet is kept from marrying a wife, the reason is plain: because with the siege close at hand all were to perish by pestilence and sword and famine, and the number of the dying would be so great that the duty of burial would be overwhelmed, and the corpses would lie like dung to be torn by birds and beasts. 5. And this too must be noted, that to waste away through sicknesses and long infirmity is the wrath of God. Hence Joram also, the son of Jehoshaphat, is consumed by infirmity, and the Apostle teaches that those who have violated holy things fall sick and waste away and die.

LXI. For thus says the Lord: Do not enter the house of feasting, nor go to lament, nor comfort them, for I have taken away my peace from this people, says the Lord, my mercy and compassions. And both great and small shall die in this land; they shall not be buried nor lamented, and they shall not cut themselves, nor shall baldness be made for them. And they shall not break bread among them for the mourner, to comfort him over the dead, and they shall not give them the drink of the cup to comfort them over their father and mother. And you shall not enter the house of feasting, to sit with them and eat and drink. 2. The Apostle commands that with those who turn away from God not even food is to be taken; and further: do not even say hail to such! And the Savior forbids the apostles to greet anyone on the way; hence Elisha too forbids Gehazi to give greetings as he went to heal the boy. Now it is the custom to bring food to those who mourn and to prepare a feast, which the Greeks call perideipna, and which among our people are commonly named 'parentalia' [ancestral rites], because rites are performed for parents. Divine Scripture also says elsewhere: give wine to those who are in mourning, that is, so that they may forget their grief. 3. The prophet is therefore commanded not to comfort anyone of the people, not to mix himself in the feasts of the enemies of God, not to perform rites over the funerals of the dead. For it is one thing to die by the common law of nature, another to be slain by the sentence of God. 'I have taken away', he says, 'my peace from this people, and they are unworthy of mercy, and I will spare no age, but both great and small shall perish alike, so that they shall lack even burial'. Nor shall they cut themselves, he says, nor shall baldness be made for them. 4. This custom existed among the ancients and still persists today among some of the Jews, that in mourning they cut their arms and make baldness; which we read Job also did. Hence the prophet is told not to break bread among them nor to go in to comfort them over the dead, not to give drink, not to enter the house of feasting, and not to mix with those who have been made ready for God's sentence. But if this is said of those who mourn, what will become of the heretics, whose word spreads like a cancer, and who daily lay low in the Church the corpses of the deceived?

LXII. For thus says the Lord of hosts, the God of Israel: Behold, I will take away from this place, before your eyes and in your days, the voice of joy and the voice of gladness, the voice of the bridegroom and the voice of the bride. 2. And when you have announced to this people all these words, and they have said to you: Why has the Lord spoken all this great evil against us? What is our iniquity, and what is our sin that we have sinned against the Lord our God?, you shall say to them: Because your fathers forsook me, says the Lord, and went after strange gods and served them and worshiped them, and forsook me and did not keep my law. But you too have done worse than your fathers. 3. When the Church has sinned, all joy and all gladness is taken away from her, of which the Apostle says: rejoice; again I say: rejoice; the voice of the bridegroom and the voice of the bride, of whom it is written: he who has the bride is the bridegroom. 'But if', he says, 'the people asks you why it suffers these things, and seeks the causes of its miseries, you shall answer them: because your fathers forsook me, says the Lord, those who preside over you in the churches, and went after strange gods — whose god is the belly, and avarice and luxury, and their glory is in their shame — and served them — for by whatever one is overcome, of that he is also the slave — and worshiped them — for each one worships what he loves — and forsook me and did not keep my law'. 4. It belongs properly to priests not only to teach the law but also to do it, so that they may teach the peoples subject to them and the flock entrusted to them not by words at all, but by examples. And lest they should perhaps say: 'it is an unjust sentence that the fathers eat the sour grape and the children's teeth are set on edge', he adds: but you too have done worse than your fathers, so that just punishments may be brought upon those who have sinned worse than their fathers.

LXIII. For behold, each one walks after the depravity of his evil heart, so as not to hear me. And I will cast you out of this land into a land which you do not know, you and your fathers, and there you shall serve strange gods day and night, who will not give you rest. 2. Once forsaken by the Lord, they do the things that are not fitting, so as to go after the desires of their evil heart, out of which come the worst thoughts; and for this reason they are separated from the Church, so as to go into a far country, which neither they nor their fathers had known before they sinned, so that there they may serve strange gods, who are not gods, but are thought to be so by the error of those who worship them. 3. But in adding 'day and night', he shows the perpetual perseverance of sinners in wickedness, since both in the days they serve shameful deeds and in the nights lust. Who will not give, he says, you rest; there is no doubt that he means the false gods, of whom he said: and there you shall serve strange gods. 4. Whatever, therefore, we sin, whatever we do by day and night and whatever evil works we commit, is the dominion of demons, who never give us rest and always drive us to add offenses to offenses and to make a heap of sins.

LXIV. Therefore, behold, the days are coming, says the Lord, and it shall no longer be said: The Lord lives, who brought the sons of Israel out of the land of Egypt, but: The Lord lives, who brought the sons of Israel out of the land of the north and out of all the lands to which I cast them; and I will bring them back into their land, which I gave to their fathers. 2. Plainly the future restoration of the people of Israel is foretold, and mercy after the captivity, which according to the letter was in part fulfilled under Zerubbabel and Jeshua the high priest and Ezra, and according to the spiritual understanding is described as to be fulfilled more truly and more perfectly in Christ. 'The time will come', he says, 'when it will no longer be said that the people was brought back from Egypt through Moses and Aaron, but that it was brought back from the land of the north when Cyrus, king of the Persians, released the captives'. 3. And out of all, he says, the lands; which will be fulfilled not at all in the time of Cyrus, but at the very end, as the Apostle says: 'after the fullness of the nations has come in, then all Israel will be saved'. We can also say this of the persecutions which befell our people from the days of Nero, of whom the Apostle writes: and I was delivered from the mouth of the lion, down to the times of Maximinus: how the Lord had mercy on his people and brought them back into their land, no doubt into the Church, which he gave to their fathers, the apostles and apostolic men.

LXV. Behold, I will send many fishers, says the Lord, and they shall fish them; and after this I will send them many hunters, and they shall hunt them from every mountain and from every hill and from the caves of the rocks, for my eyes are upon all their ways. They are not hidden from my face, and their iniquity was not concealed from my eyes. And I will repay first their double iniquities and sins, with which they have defiled my land with the carcasses of their idols and have filled my inheritance with their abominations. 2. The interpretation of this passage is diverse. For the Jews suppose that the Chaldeans are signified, who are described under the name of fishers, and afterwards the Romans, who are compared to hunters and who hunted the unhappy people from the mountains and hills and caves of the rocks. And the Lord says that he himself did this because he looked upon their ways and repaid their iniquities, with which they had defiled the land by venerating images, and had polluted his inheritance with the abominations of idols. 3. But our writers more rightly and better think that the prophet prophesied these things about the future. For since he had said above: I will bring them back into their land, which I gave to their fathers, he now shows how they are to be brought back: that first he sends the apostles, to whom the Savior said: come after me, and I will make you fishers of men, and afterwards hunters, whom we can take to be either churchmen or angels, who, once the time of the consummation has come, are to hunt all the saints from the mountains of lofty doctrines and from the hills of good works and from the caves of the rocks, the apostles and apostolic men. 4. For not only is Christ the rock, but he also granted to the apostle Peter that he should be called rock; those who rest in his thoughts are rightly said to have been transferred from the rock. And he shows that either the apostles or those who are afterwards to be hunted had sins and received their double iniquities. For the servant who knows the will of his lord and does not do it shall be beaten much. And it must be known that in the Hebrew 'first' is set down, and in the edition of the Seventy it is omitted. 5. But when he says: I will repay first their double iniquities and sins, he intimates that after they have received evils, they will receive good things too. And these who are afterwards to be transferred have defiled the land of the Lord with the carcasses of their idols and have filled his inheritance with their abominations, so that the whole world may become subject to God and be preserved not at all by its own merit, but by his mercy. What we have set down here from the Hebrew: they are not hidden from my face, is not found in the Seventy.

LXVI. O Lord, my strength and my might and my refuge in the day of tribulation or of evils! All the strength of men without the power of God, which is Christ, is reckoned feeble and of no account; hence one must flee to God and say: Lord, you have become a refuge for us from generation to generation; and in another place: he who dwells in the help of the Most High shall abide in the protection of the God of heaven. 2. And the day of tribulation or of evils is to be understood as that of which the Apostle also says: that he might rescue us from the present evil age, and: redeeming the time, because the days are evil.

LXVII. To you the nations shall come from the ends of the earth and shall say: Truly our fathers possessed a lie, vanity, which did not profit them. LXX: to you the nations shall come from the ends of the earth and shall say: How false are the idols our fathers possessed, and there is no profit in them! 2. After Israel has been cast out and carried away by the fishers and hunters, the multitude of the nations is accordingly called to faith and confesses that it, or its fathers, had been engaged in their former error. And those who say: 'how false are the idols our fathers possessed, in which there is no profit!' confess that the things to which they are passing over are true and supported by every protection.

LXVIII. Shall a man make gods for himself, and they are not gods? This too the nations say, who have come to the Savior from the ends of the earth, rebuking the ignorance both of themselves and of their ancestors, by which they thought that gods are made by man, when it belongs to God to make men.

LXIX. Therefore, behold, I will show them at this turn, I will show them my hand and my power, and they shall know that my name is the Lord. The hand of God, through which he has wrought all things, and the power, of which the Apostle said: 'Christ the power of God and the wisdom of God', is shown to the nations once the passion of the Son has been completed. 2. And beautifully he said: 'I will show them at this turn; and I will show them plainly, and not as before in shadow and in image and in prophecy of things to come, so that, after they have come to know it, they may know my name and hear from the Son: Father, I have made your name manifest to men'.

Jeremiah 17

LXX.

The sin of Judah is written with an iron stylus, with an adamantine nail, engraved upon the breadth of their heart — or on the breast of their heart — and on the horns of the altars — or altar-stones — of them. Concerning the nations which had been converted to the Lord, it was said above: behold, I will show them at this turn, I will show them my hand and my power; now he speaks of Israel, which has been cast off: the sin of Judah is written with an iron stylus, with an adamantine nail, and so on. 2. Why this was passed over by the Seventy I do not know, unless perhaps they spared their own people, as it is evident that they did also in Isaiah: cease therefore from man, whose breath is in his nostrils, because he himself has been reckoned exalted; and many things of this kind, for which, if I wished to set them all out, there would be need, I will not say of a book, but of books. The sins of the nations have been blotted out, because, converted to the Lord from the ends of the earth, they have heard this: praise the Lord, all you nations, praise him, all you peoples, because his mercy has been confirmed upon us, and the truth of the Lord remains forever; and they are those concerning whom he had said to Moses: let me alone, that I may kill this people, and I will make you into a great nation. 3. But the sin of Judah, indelible and, so to speak, to be abolished by no means, is written with an iron stylus, with an adamantine nail, which in Hebrew is called 'samir': not that there is any nail which is called 'samir', but because the adamant stone, which received this name from the fact that it is untamed and unbreakable, has such polish and smoothness that one can write on it with an iron stylus without any hindrance, so that the hard substance of iron writes on a harder tablet of adamant, and what is written lasts forever. For they themselves said: his blood be upon us and upon our children. 4. Hence it is also engraved or carved on the horns of the altars — or altar-stones — of them, so that the works of sacrilege may endure in eternal memory. But if this is so, where is that which the doting old woman invents, that a man can be without sin if he wishes, and that the commandments of God are easy?

LXXI. When their sons remember their altar-stones and their groves and leafy trees on the high mountains, sacrificing in the field, I will give your strength and all your treasures for plunder, your high places on account of sins in all your borders. And you shall be left alone, bereft of your inheritance, which I gave you, and I will make you serve your enemies in a land which you do not know, since you have kindled a fire in my fury; it will burn forever. 2. These things too are not found in the Seventy, for the same reason, I think, that we stated above, namely, lest an eternal sentence should remain against them.
